# Weighted dilation graphs, smooth shifted primes and totient fibers

Coordinate `EN-101-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-C-AL-H-0031` | `PM-011.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Weighted-Dilation-Graphs-Smooth-Shifted-Primes-and-Totient-Fibers-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint Weighted-Dilation-Graphs-Smooth-Shifted-Primes-and-Totient-Fibers-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove Erdős's conjecture on the largest fibers of Euler's totient function: for every <i>ε</i> &gt; 0, infinitely many positive integers <i>n</i> have more than $`n^{1-\\varepsilon }`$ preimages. We also show that, for every fixed <i>δ</i> &gt; 0, there are at least $`x^{1-o(1)}`$ primes <i>p</i> in $`2x\\lt p\\le5x`$ whose predecessors have no prime factor exceeding <i>x</i><sup><i>δ</i></sup>.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Erdős's conjecture on the largest fibers of Euler's totient function: for every <i>ε</i> &gt; 0, infinitely many positive integers <i>n</i> have more than $`n^{1-\varepsilon }`$ preimages. We also show that, for every fixed <i>δ</i> &gt; 0, there are at least $`x^{1-o(1)}`$ primes <i>p</i> in $`2x\lt p\le5x`$ whose predecessors have no prime factor exceeding <i>x</i><sup><i>δ</i></sup>.

- **claim-totient-fibers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-totient)): For every real $\varepsilon>0$, there are infinitely many positive integers $n$ such that $$g(n)>n^{1-\varepsilon}.$$
  - Explanation: For every positive exponent deficit, infinitely many integers have a totient fiber larger than the corresponding power of the integer, which settles the conjecture on the largest fibers.
- **claim-smooth-shifted-primes** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-smooth)): For every fixed $0<\delta<1/4$, as $x\to\infty$, $$\label{eq:main-smooth} \#\{p:\ p\text{ prime},\ 2x<p\le5x, \ P^+(p-1)\le x^\delta\} \ge x^{1-o(1)}.$$ The quantity $o(1)$ may depend on $\delta$.
  - Explanation: In the window between two and five times x, at least x to the power one minus a vanishing exponent primes have a predecessor with only small prime factors.
- **claim-siegel-walfisz** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-sw)): For fixed $A,C>0$, uniformly for $(a,r)=1$ and $1\le r\le(\log y)^C$, $$\#\{p\le y:p\equiv a\pmod r\} =\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Li}}(y)}{\varphi(r)} +O_{A,C}\!\left(\frac{y}{(\log y)^A}\right).$$ The constants, which need not be effective, are independent of $y,a,r$. [thm:ideal](#thm:ideal)
  - Explanation: Primes in a reduced residue class are equidistributed up to a logarithmic power saving, uniformly for moduli bounded by a power of the logarithm; constants need not be effective.
- **claim-large-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-large-sieve)): Let $I$ be a real interval of length $H$, let $a_n$ be complex numbers supported on $I\cap\mathbb Z$, and let $R\ge1$. Then $$\sum_{r\le R}\frac r{\varphi(r)} \sum_{\chi\bmod r}^{*} \left\|\sum_{n\in I\cap\mathbb Z}a_n\chi(n)\right\|^2 \ll (H+1+R^2)\sum_n\|a_n\|^2,$$ where the asterisk restricts the sum to primitive Dirichlet characters.
  - Explanation: A mean-square bound over primitive characters of all moduli up to a cutoff, controlled by the interval length, the cutoff squared and the coefficient energy.
- **claim-harmonic-character-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-harmonic-sw)): Fix $c,C,B,A>0$. If $\chi$ is nonprincipal modulo $r\le L^B$, then, uniformly for real $\|t\|\le L^B$ and intervals $I\subset[\exp(L^c),x^C]$, $$\sum_{p\in I}\frac{\chi(p)p^{it}}p\ll_{c,C,B,A}L^{-A}.$$ The same conclusion holds for a nonprincipal character induced from a smaller modulus.
  - Explanation: Harmonic sums of a nonprincipal character twisted by a power of the prime saving any fixed logarithmic power, even on very short prime intervals.
- **claim-divisor-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-divisor-moments)): For each fixed nonnegative integer $k$, $$\sum_{n\le Y}\frac{\tau(n)^k}{n}\ll_k(\log(2Y))^{C_k}, \qquad \sum_{n\le Y}\tau(n)^k\ll_k Y(\log(2Y))^{C_k}$$ for a constant $C_k$. Consequently, a convolution of a fixed number of sequences bounded by fixed logarithmic powers has coefficient-square sum $U L^{O(1)}$ on a dyad of size $U\le x^{O(1)}$. If its coefficients include the reciprocal of the product index, this bound is $L^{O(1)}/U$ instead.
  - Explanation: Fixed powers of the divisor function have logarithmic-power harmonic and counting sums, which controls coefficient energy of convolutions of log-bounded sequences.
- **claim-dirichlet-mean-square** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dirichlet-mean)): If $P(t)=\sum_{n\le Y}c_n n^{it}$ and $I$ is an interval of length $H$, then $$\int_I\|P(t)\|^2\,dt \ll \bigl(H+Y\log(2Y)\bigr)\sum_n\|c_n\|^2.$$ For a set $\mathcal T$ of real points at mutual distance at least one, contained in an interval of length $H$, one also has $$\label{eq:discrete-mean} \sum_{t\in\mathcal T}\|P(t)\|^2 \ll \bigl(H+1+Y\log(2Y)\bigr)(\log(2Y))^2 \sum_n\|c_n\|^2.$$ The constants are absolute, independently of any factorization used to form the coefficients $c_n$.
  - Explanation: Continuous and discrete mean-square bounds for Dirichlet polynomials with absolute constants, independent of how the coefficients arise.
- **claim-derivative-tests** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-derivative-tests)): Let $f$ be real-valued on an interval containing $H$ consecutive integers.* 1. *If $f'$ is monotone and stays in $[j+\lambda,j+1-\lambda]$ for some integer $j$ and $0<\lambda\le1/2$, then $\sum_n e(f(n))\ll\lambda^{-1}$.* 2. *If $f$ is twice continuously differentiable and $\lambda\le\|f''\|\le C\lambda$ throughout the interval, then $\sum_n e(f(n))\ll_C H\lambda^{1/2}+\lambda^{-1/2}$.* *Both estimates hold on every subinterval on which the stated hypotheses hold.
  - Explanation: First and second derivative tests give cancellation in exponential sums over consecutive integers, valid on subintervals where the hypotheses hold.
- **claim-fourier-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-separation)): Let $F$ be smooth, supported in a fixed bounded box in $\mathbb R^b$, where $b$ is fixed. Suppose that for every nonnegative integer $j$ its derivatives of order $j$ are bounded by $C_jL^{C(j+1)}$. Then Fourier inversion separates the variables with integrated absolute coefficient mass $L^{O(1)}$. There is a fixed $C'>0$, depending on $b,C$, such that restricting each Fourier frequency to absolute value at most $L^{C'}$ leaves an error $O_A(L^{-A})$ for every fixed $A$. The exponent $C'$ may be fixed before the desired $A$.
  - Explanation: A smooth cutoff with controlled derivatives splits into separated phases with polylogarithmic coefficient mass, after truncating frequencies at a fixed power of the logarithm.
- **claim-block-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sieve)): Consider a finite set of objects with nonnegative weights. For some primes $p\le z$, let a bad condition be specified. If $d$ is a squarefree product of these primes, suppose the weight of the objects on which all conditions at $p\mid d$ hold is $$Xg(d)+r(d),$$ including $d=1$, where $X\ge0$, $g$ is multiplicative, and $$\label{eq:sieve-density-hypotheses} 0\le g(p)\le1-\eta_0,\qquad \sum_{w<p\le w^2}g(p)\le C\quad(w>1)$$ for fixed $\eta_0>0$ and $C$. For every sufficiently large even integer $h$, the weight $S$ of objects avoiding all bad conditions satisfies $$\label{eq:block-sieve} S=X\prod_{p\le z}(1-g(p))\bigl(1+O(e^{-h})\bigr) +O\!\left(\sum_{d\le z^{4h+2}}\|r(d)\|\right).$$ Only the indicated primes and their squarefree products are used. The implied constants depend on $\eta_0,C$, uniformly also when $h$ grows. For the fixed even choice $h=2$, there is an upper bound by a constant times $X\prod(1-g(p))$ plus the same remainder sum.* *More precisely, the proof supplies polynomials $U$ and $V$ in the indicators of the bad conditions such that $$V\le\mathbf 1_{\text{no bad condition}}\le U,\qquad U\ge0.$$ Both have coefficients of absolute value at most one, supported on squarefree $d\le z^{4h+2}$. The nonnegative overcount $U-\mathbf 1_{\text{no bad condition}}$ is dominated by the pointwise nonnegative polynomial $U-V$, whose absolute coefficients are also at most one and which has the same level bound.
  - Explanation: A Brun–Hooley type sieve with explicit upper and lower polynomials of bounded coefficients and controlled level, giving the sieve main term with a relative error exponentially small in the parameter h.
- **claim-rough-sieve-polynomial** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rough-sieve-polynomial)): For the ordinary bad conditions $p\mid n$ at primes $p\le z$, let $U(n)=\sum_{d\mid n}\lambda_d$ be the upper polynomial in [eq:sieve-polynomials](#eq:sieve-polynomials), and put $D=z^{4h+2}$. Then $$U(n)\ge\mathbf 1_{P^{-}(n)>z}\ge0,\quad \|\lambda_d\|\le1,\quad \lambda_d=0\quad\text{unless $d\le D$ is squarefree},$$ and $$\label{eq:rough-sieve-overcount} \sum_{n\in I\cap\mathbb N} \bigl(U(n)-\mathbf 1_{P^{-}(n)>z}\bigr) \ll \|I\|e^{-h}+D$$ for every finite interval $I\subset[1,\infty)$ and all sufficiently large even $h$. The bound is uniform in $h,z,I$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:rough-sieve-harmonic} \sum_d\frac{\|\lambda_d\|}{d} \le\prod_{p\le z}(1+p^{-1})\ll\log(2z).$$
  - Explanation: For rough integers the upper sieve polynomial is a divisor-sum majorant with bounded coefficients, small overcount on intervals and harmonic cost of logarithmic size.
- **claim-physical-schur** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-physical-schur)): Replace the coefficients of each row transition of $\mathcal A$ by their absolute values before adding patterns or averaging permutations. The resulting operator and its adjoint have row sums at most $L^{C_{\rm abs}}$, where $C_{\rm abs}$ may depend on $m,C_1$ and the fixed data in [def:prime-groups](#def:prime-groups), but is independent of $J$. The same assertion holds if the position is replaced by independent uniform residues at all group primes.
  - Explanation: The absolute-value version of the physical operator and its adjoint have row sums bounded by a log power with exponent independent of the slot count J.
- **claim-local-transference** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-transference)): For every fixed $E>0$ there is $E_{\rm id}>0$, depending only on $E$ and the fixed data in [def:prime-groups](#def:prime-groups), with the following property. Assume $$\label{eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis} \sup_{\Theta\in\mathbb R}\lVert \mathcal T(\Theta)\rVert\le L^{-E_{\rm id}}.$$ Then, for every sufficiently large fixed $J$, where the lower bound on $J$ may also depend on $m,C_1$, put $$R=\lceil L^{0.52}\rceil,\qquad N=2R.$$ For every fixed $\gamma>0$ and $I=[X,2X)$ with $x^\gamma\le X\le x^{1/\gamma}$, one has $$\label{eq:transference-moment} \frac1{\|I\cap\mathbb Z\|}\sum_{n\in I\cap\mathbb Z} \langle u_n,(\mathcal A\mathcal A^*)^Ru_n\rangle \le L^{-EN},$$ for all sufficiently large $x$. Here $u_n$ is one on every physical state at position $n$ and zero elsewhere. The threshold for $x$ may depend on all fixed parameters, including $J,C_2, \gamma$, and the bound is uniform subject to these parameters and [eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis](#eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis). No bound on $\zeta$ is needed apart from the assumed ideal estimate.
  - Explanation: A small ideal operator norm for independent labels implies a strong averaged high-moment bound for the physical dilation graph, with the order of parameter choices made explicit.
- **claim-uniform-residue-replacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transference-crt)): For each compatible fixed path and every fixed $A>0$, the average of [eq:path-residue-factor](#eq:path-residue-factor) over $I\cap\mathbb Z$ equals its expectation under independent uniform residues at all group primes, multiplied by $1+O(\exp(-ANT))$. Incompatible active congruences give zero in both models.
  - Explanation: Averaging the path factor over an interval equals its expectation under independent uniform residues, up to a relative error beating any prescribed power; incompatible congruences vanish in both models.
- **claim-memory-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-memory-identity)): With the distinct-birth rule, the independent-residue path expression with the phase in [eq:return-fourier](#eq:return-fourier) equals $$\label{eq:memory-product} \left(\prod_{p\ \text{big}}b_p\right) \langle\mathbf1_\varnothing, G_0\mathcal E_0G_1\cdots\mathcal E_{N-1}G_N \mathbf1_\varnothing\rangle_{\rm distinct}.$$ The subscript means that the indicator is inserted in the expanded path integral, not that each local operator separately enforces it.
  - Explanation: Under the distinct-birth rule the independent-residue path expression equals a boundary matrix element of an alternating product of visit and edge operators on the memory space.
- **claim-memory-absolute-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-memory-absolute)): There is $C_a$, allowed to depend on $m,C_1$ but independent of $J$, such that every absolute edge has weighted row and column bounds $L^{C_a}$. These bounds and [eq:ghost-absolute-bound](#eq:ghost-absolute-bound) persist under arbitrary multipliers of modulus at most one on individual local transition choices, and under memory-size projections.
  - Explanation: Absolute edge operators have weighted row and column bounds of a log power independent of J, stable under unimodular local multipliers and memory-size projections.
- **claim-rare-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rare-transfer)): With the memory truncation in [eq:memory-truncation](#eq:memory-truncation), the aggregate of all dirty terms of any edge has norm at most $$\label{eq:dirty-norm} 2L^{C_a}\left(\frac B{\mathcal D}\right)^{1/2},$$ after enlarging $C_a$ independently of $J$ if necessary.
  - Explanation: Dirty edge terms, those that store or promote a particle, have small norm because few of the equally likely omitted small labels permit any promotion.
- **claim-clean-norm** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-clean-norm)): There is $C_s$, depending only on the fixed data in [def:prime-groups](#def:prime-groups), such that the clean part of every edge satisfies $$\label{eq:clean-norm} \lVert \mathcal E_{i,\rm clean}\rVert \le 2L^{-E_{\rm id}+C_s}$$ for all sufficiently large $x$, under [eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis](#eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis). In particular $C_s$ is independent of $m,C_1,J$.
  - Explanation: The clean part of every edge inherits the power saving of the ideal operator, with loss exponent independent of m, C_1 and J.
- **claim-transference-pairing** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-transference-pairing)): Under the hypotheses and choices of [thm:transference](#thm:transference), let $f\in\mathcal H_{\rm ph}$ be supported on positions $I=[X,2X)$ and independent of the chosen marks, with value $f_n$ at position $n$. Suppose $$\sup_{n\in I}\|f_n\|\le\exp(O(\sqrt L)),\qquad \lVert f\rVert,\lVert g\rVert\le X^{1/2}L^{C_3}.$$ Then $$\label{eq:transference-pairing} \|\langle f,\mathcal A g\rangle\| \ll XL^{-E+2C_3}.$$ Only the first endpoint must be independent of its mark list.
  - Explanation: The root moment bound yields cancellation in a pairing whose first endpoint is independent of the mark lists; the second endpoint needs only a norm bound.
- **claim-ideal-operator-small-norm** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-ideal)): Fix $E_{\rm id}>0$ and a fixed frequency exponent $C_4\geq0$. There are fixed integers $m,J_0$ and a fixed constant $C_1$, depending only on these parameters, $\ell$, and the prime-group bounds, with the following property. For every fixed $J\geq J_0$, there is a family consisting of the raw pattern $$C_g=\varnothing\quad\hbox{for every }g,\qquad K_0=1,$$ and signed comparison patterns satisfying $$C_g=\varnothing\ (g\in\mathcal S),\qquad \|C_g\|\leq m\ (g\in\mathcal B),\qquad \sum_\nu\sup\|K_\nu\|\leq L^{C_1},$$ such that the symmetrized ideal operator of [sec:transference](#sec:transference) satisfies $$\label{eq:ideal-small-norm} \sup_{\Theta\in\mathbb R,\ \|\zeta\|\leq L^{C_4}} \lVert \mathcal T(\Theta)\rVert_{2\to2}\leq L^{-E_{\rm id}}$$ for sufficiently large $x$. The coefficients and the family are independent of $\Theta$ and of the individual value of $\zeta$. Their defining parameters are independent of $J$; $J$ only supplies the available shared slots. The threshold for $x$ may depend on $J$.* *More precisely, designate $m$ of the first $J$ slots in each big group as probes, and split the big groups into two blocks. Each comparison frees nonempty sets $A$ and $B$ of designated probes in the respective blocks, and its coefficient has the form $$\label{eq:ideal-comparison-coefficient} -(-1)^{\|A\|+\|B\|}\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A) \mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B).$$ Here $D_A,D_B$ are the products of the free labels used to form $D$, $X_A$ is the product of the target labels in $A$, and $X_B$ the product of the input labels in $B$. These coefficients use no shared labels and none of the last $\ell$ labels. Each kernel is a finite sum of products of matched log-cell indicators and pairs of Dirichlet characters of conductor bounded by a fixed power of $L$.
  - Explanation: A raw pattern together with signed comparison patterns of bounded total coefficient mass makes the symmetrized ideal operator small at every additive frequency and every Mellin frequency in range.
- **claim-integer-bilinear** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bilinear)): Let $U,V\geq1$. Suppose $a_n,b_t$ are supported in integer intervals of lengths at most $C U,C V$, respectively, where $C$ is fixed. If $$\alpha=u/r+\beta,\qquad (u,r)=1,\quad r\geq1, \qquad \|\beta\|\leq r^{-2},$$ then $$\label{eq:integer-bilinear} \left\|\sum_{n,t}a_nb_te\!\left(\alpha nt\right)\right\| \ll_C \lVert a\rVert_{\ell^2}\lVert b\rVert_{\ell^2} \left[U+(1+V/r)\{U+r\log(2r)\}\right]^{1/2}.$$ The containing intervals need not begin at $1$, and the coefficients may vanish on arbitrary subsets of them.
  - Explanation: A bilinear exponential sum with phase near a rational with small denominator is bounded by a product of coefficient norms times an explicit factor depending on the scales and denominator.
- **claim-comparison-kernel** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-kernel)): Fix $m$, $A_0>0$, and $C_4\geq0$. There are fixed powers $Q,h^{-1},\xi^{-1}$ of $L$, independent of $J$, such that the kernels in [eq:ideal-cell-kernel](#eq:ideal-cell-kernel) have the following property. Let $A,B$ be nonempty sets of slots, using at most $m$ slots per big group. Take independent label tuples for $D_A,X_A,D_B,X_B$ with the prescribed slot laws. If $f$ and $g$ are arbitrary $L^2$ tests on the tuples making up $X_B$ and $X_A$, respectively, then for every $\alpha\in\mathbb R$ and $\|\zeta\|\leq L^{C_4}$, $$\label{eq:ideal-comparison} \begin{split} \Big\|\mathbb E\,\overline{f(X_B)}g(X_A) \big[&(X_AX_B)^{i\zeta}e\!\left(\alpha X_AX_B\right)\\ &-\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B) (D_AD_B)^{i\zeta}e\!\left(\alpha D_AD_B\right)\big]\Big\| \leq L^{-A_0}\lVert f\rVert_2\lVert g\rVert_2. \end{split}$$ Here notation such as $f(X_B)$ denotes a tuple test, not an assumption of dependence only on the product. The kernels are independent of $\alpha$ and $\zeta$.
  - Explanation: Log-cell character kernels replace a coupled product phase by independent products, with error smaller than any fixed negative log power, uniformly in the frequencies.
- **claim-lifted-shift-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-shift)): Fix all the group and endpoint bounds above. Fix $D_0,C>0$ and a fixed compact interval $[c_\Psi,C_\Psi]\subset(0,\infty)$. Suppose $0<\|k\|\leq L^C$ is integral, $xL^{-C}\leq Z\leq xL^C$, $\|a_1\|,\|a_2\|\leq L^C$, and $\Psi$ is supported in that interval with $\\|\Psi^{(j)}\\|_\infty\ll_j L^{C(j+1)}$ for every $j\geq0$. There exists a fixed $B$, depending only on these bounds and $D_0$, such that [eq:discrepancy](#eq:discrepancy) implies $$\label{eq:shift-bound} \sum_{\substack{z>0\\z+k>0}} \frac{\Psi(z/Z)}z\,z^{ia_1}(z+k)^{ia_2} \overline{F_0(z)}G_0(z+k)W_\ell(z)W_\ell(z+k) \ll L^{-D_0}.$$ The bound is uniform over the indicated sequences, intervals, characters, frequencies, and cutoffs. It also holds when the discrepancy-bearing endpoint or its coefficients have been conjugated.
  - Explanation: Shifted correlations of two endpoint functions weighted by marked divisor weights have arbitrary logarithmic saving once one endpoint satisfies a sufficiently strong discrepancy condition.
- **claim-exceptional-times** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exceptional-times)): The set $\mathcal E$ meets at most $$\exp\!\left(O(L^b+L^{1-a}\log L)\right)=Y^{o(1)}$$ intervals $[j,j+1]$, uniformly in the bounded coefficients $b_s$.
  - Explanation: The set where the small-prime polynomial is large meets only a subpower number of unit intervals, proved by high moments and a Sobolev bound.
- **claim-sparse-residual** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-residual)): For the occupied unit intervals $I_j$ in [lem:exceptional-times](#lem:exceptional-times), $$\sum_j\sup_{t\in I_j}\|\mathcal R(t)\|^2\ll L^{C_{12}}.$$
  - Explanation: A sparse mean-square bound for the residual polynomial over the occupied unit intervals, by a Gram-matrix row-sum argument.
- **claim-long-rough-polynomial** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-long-rough-polynomial)): Fix $\tau>0$, $\eta<1/4$, $C>0$, and $A>0$. Let $x^\tau/2\leq P'\leq x^\eta$, let $J'\subset[P',2P']$ be an interval, and let $\chi$ have modulus at most $L^C$. If $\|\sigma\|\leq L^C$, then, for a sufficiently large fixed $B_0$, uniformly whenever $L^{B_0}\leq\|t\|$ and $\|t+\sigma\|\leq x^2$, $$\left\|\sum_{\substack{n\in J'\\P^{-}(n)>W}} \chi(n)n^{-1+i(t+\sigma)}\right\|\ll L^{-A}.$$ The constant $B_0$ can be chosen after $C,A,\tau,\eta$.
  - Explanation: A character-twisted sum over rough integers in a long interval saves any fixed log power at large frequencies, via the sieve majorant and iterated van der Corput differencing.
- **claim-type-ii** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-typeii)): Fix $D_*>0$, $b_*>0$, and $C>0$, and require $\eta_*<b_*/4$ in [eq:typeii-rough-slot](#eq:typeii-rough-slot). Let $U,V$ be dyadic scales satisfying $$UV\asymp x,\qquad x^{b_*}\leq U,V\leq x^{1-b_*}.$$ Let $\Psi$ be a fixed smooth function with compact support in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$. Suppose that $\lvert \alpha_m\rvert,\lvert \beta_n\rvert\leq L^C$, and that $\alpha_m=0$ unless $P^{-}(m)>W$. There is a fixed exponent $B$, depending only on these data and the fixed group and band constants, such that the discrepancy hypothesis in [eq:discrepancy](#eq:discrepancy) on $\alpha$ implies $$\label{eq:typeii-bound} \sum_{\substack{m\asymp U,\ n\asymp V,\ u\geq1\\mn=2u+1}} A(u)\Psi(u/x)\alpha_m\beta_n \ll xL^{-D_*}.$$ Here the sequence to which the discrepancy hypothesis is applied includes the actual restriction $m\asymp U$ and any additional interval restriction on $m$. Explicitly, for that restricted sequence one requires $$\left\|\sum_{m\in I}\frac{\alpha_m\chi(m)m^{it}}m\right\| \leq L^{-B} \quad\left(I\subset[1,x^2],\quad \operatorname{mod}(\chi)\leq L^B,\quad \|t\|\leq L^B\right).$$ There is no discrepancy hypothesis on $\beta$. The constants are uniform over the sequences, scales, and interval restrictions satisfying these conditions.
  - Explanation: The bilinear sum over factorizations of the shifted integer against the band weight cancels with log power saving when one coefficient sequence satisfies the discrepancy hypothesis.
- **claim-typeii-order-of-precisions** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rem-typeii-order)): The band constants, $j_{**}$, $b_*$, coefficient bound $C$, and target $D_*$ are fixed first. The transition functions, their total variation, and the coefficient and diagonal exponents are independent of $K_0$; only then is $K_0$ chosen. The smooth separation after Cauchy’s inequality and the accuracy required from [thm:shift](#thm:shift) are fixed next, and the required discrepancy exponent $B$ is fixed last. Accordingly, when $\alpha$ is the difference between a test and its rough-integer proxy, the precision of the cells defining that proxy may be chosen after $B$, provided the proxy’s coefficient bound is already uniform in that cell precision. This is the order used in [sec:primes](#sec:primes).
  - Explanation: Records the order in which the constants of the Type II argument are fixed, so that the proxy cell precision can be chosen after the discrepancy exponent without circularity.
- **claim-candidate-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-candidate-mass)): Put $$X_A=\sum_u A(u)\Psi(u/x),\quad H_Q=\sum_v\frac{a_Q(v)}v,\quad H_P=\sum_{r:r_*=1}\frac{W_1(r)}r.$$ There are constants $C,C_A$ depending only on the early candidate choices such that, for sufficiently large $x$ after fixing $j_{**}$, $$\begin{aligned} 0\le A(u)&\le L^C\tau(u_*), & A(u)&\le\exp(C\sqrt L)\quad(u\asymp x),\label{eq:candidate-pointwise}\\ H_P&\sim\exp\left(q\sum_g V_g\right), & X_A&\asymp\frac{x}{L}H_QH_P\gg xL^{-C_A}. \label{eq:candidate-mass} \end{aligned}$$ The comparison constants in <a href="#eq:candidate-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:candidate-mass">[eq:candidate-mass]</a> can be chosen independently of $j_{**}\ge j_0$. On the support of $A(u)\Psi(u/x)$ every prime divisor of $u$ lies in $[L^{20},x^\delta]$.
  - Explanation: The candidate weight has pointwise bounds and total mass of the expected order, with constants independent of the placement of the rough slot.
- **claim-type-i-distribution** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-typei)): For every fixed $\vartheta<1/2$ and $D>0$, $$\label{eq:typeI-bound} \sum_{\substack{d\le x^\vartheta\\d\text{ odd and squarefree}}} \|r(d)\|\ll xL^{-D}+X_AL^{-18}.$$
  - Explanation: The remainders in congruence counts for the candidate, summed over odd squarefree moduli below a level under one half, are small.
- **claim-proxy-discrepancy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-proxy-discrepancy)): The denominators in <a href="#eq:proxy-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:proxy-definition">[eq:proxy-definition]</a> are positive for large $x$. Moreover $0\le B_I\le1$. Given any fixed discrepancy exponent $B$, taking $S>2B+3$ makes $$\alpha_m=(I(m)-B_I(m))\mathbf 1_{m\in K}$$ satisfy <a href="#eq:discrepancy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:discrepancy">[eq:discrepancy]</a>, uniformly for every interval $K$ inside a dyad in the stated factor ranges, for each of the finitely many tests above. Thus the coefficient bound required by [thm:typeii](#thm:typeii) is independent of $S$.
  - Explanation: The difference between a test and its cell-constant rough-integer proxy satisfies the discrepancy condition once the cell precision is fine enough, with coefficient bound independent of that precision.
- **claim-proxy-densities** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-proxy-densities)): For the prime proxy in the interval $x^{1/2-2\kappa}\le m\le x^{1/2+2\kappa}$, $$\label{eq:prime-proxy-size} c_{\mathrm{pr}}(m)\le\frac{C}{LV(W)},$$ where $C$ is absolute for $0<\kappa<1/50$. If $mn=N_u$ on the support of $\Psi(u/x)$ and $\log n/L\in(\gamma,\gamma']$, then $$\label{eq:rough-proxy-size} c_\gamma(m)\le \frac{\sup_{\gamma\le a\le\gamma'}D_\gamma(1-a)+o(1)}{LV(W)}.$$ At fixed $b_1$ one also has $$\begin{aligned} \int_{b_1}^{1/2}D_\alpha(1-\alpha)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha} &=D_{b_1}(1)-1,\label{eq:density-integral}\\ D_{b_1}(1)&\le\frac{e^{-\gamma_E}}{b_1} (1+O(e^{-c/b_1})).\label{eq:density-sieve-upper} \end{aligned}$$ The integrand at the endpoint $\alpha=1/2$ is understood by its left limit; changing that endpoint value has no effect.
  - Explanation: Upper bounds for the prime and rough-integer proxies in terms of Buchstab-type densities, an exact integral identity from the minimum-coordinate decomposition, and a sieve upper bound for the density.
- **claim-finite-totient-fibers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-totient-fibers)): For each positive integer $n$, the set of positive integers $m$ with $\varphi(m)=n$ is finite. For every $\eta>0$, $$g(n)\ll_\eta n^{1+\eta}.$$
  - Explanation: Every totient fiber is finite and its size is at most a power of n with exponent arbitrarily close to one.

```json
        "exact_quote": "If $P(t)=\\sum_{n\\le Y}c_n n^{it}$ and $I$ is an interval of length $H$, then $$\\int_I|P(t)|^2\\,dt\n \\ll \\bigl(H+Y\\log(2Y)\\bigr)\\sum_n|c_n|^2.$$ For a set $\\mathcal T$ of real points at mutual distance at least one, contained in an interval of length $H$, one also has $$\\label{eq:discrete-mean}\n \\sum_{t\\in\\mathcal T}|P(t)|^2\n \\ll \\bigl(H+1+Y\\log(2Y)\\bigr)(\\log(2Y))^2\n       \\sum_n|c_n|^2.$$ The constants are absolute, independently of any factorization used to form the coefficients $c_n$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be smooth, supported in a fixed bounded box in $\\mathbb R^b$, where $b$ is fixed. Suppose that for every nonnegative integer $j$ its derivatives of order $j$ are bounded by $C_jL^{C(j+1)}$. Then Fourier inversion separates the variables with integrated absolute coefficient mass $L^{O(1)}$. There is a fixed $C'>0$, depending on $b,C$, such that restricting each Fourier frequency to absolute value at most $L^{C'}$ leaves an error $O_A(L^{-A})$ for every fixed $A$. The exponent $C'$ may be fixed before the desired $A$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sieve",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a finite set of objects with nonnegative weights. For some primes $p\\le z$, let a bad condition be specified. If $d$ is a squarefree product of these primes, suppose the weight of the objects on which all conditions at $p\\mid d$ hold is $$Xg(d)+r(d),$$ including $d=1$, where $X\\ge0$, $g$ is multiplicative, and $$\\label{eq:sieve-density-hypotheses}\n 0\\le g(p)\\le1-\\eta_0,\\qquad\n \\sum_{w<p\\le w^2}g(p)\\le C\\quad(w>1)$$ for fixed $\\eta_0>0$ and $C$. For every sufficiently large even integer $h$, the weight $S$ of objects avoiding all bad conditions satisfies $$\\label{eq:block-sieve}\n S=X\\prod_{p\\le z}(1-g(p))\\bigl(1+O(e^{-h})\\bigr)\n   +O\\!\\left(\\sum_{d\\le z^{4h+2}}|r(d)|\\right).$$ Only the indicated primes and their squarefree products are used. The implied constants depend on $\\eta_0,C$, uniformly also when $h$ grows. For the fixed even choice $h=2$, there is an upper bound by a constant times $X\\prod(1-g(p))$ plus the same remainder sum.*\n\n*More precisely, the proof supplies polynomials $U$ and $V$ in the indicators of the bad conditions such that $$V\\le\\mathbf 1_{\\text{no bad condition}}\\le U,\\qquad U\\ge0.$$ Both have coefficients of absolute value at most one, supported on squarefree $d\\le z^{4h+2}$. The nonnegative overcount $U-\\mathbf 1_{\\text{no bad condition}}$ is dominated by the pointwise nonnegative polynomial $U-V$, whose absolute coefficients are also at most one and which has the same level bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sieve",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a finite set of objects with nonnegative weights. For some primes $p\\le z$, let a bad condition be specified. If $d$ is a squarefree product of these primes, suppose the weight of the objects on which all conditions at $p\\mid d$ hold is $$Xg(d)+r(d),$$ including $d=1$, where $X\\ge0$, $g$ is multiplicative, and $$\\label{eq:sieve-density-hypotheses}\n 0\\le g(p)\\le1-\\eta_0,\\qquad\n \\sum_{w<p\\le w^2}g(p)\\le C\\quad(w>1)$$ for fixed $\\eta_0>0$ and $C$. For every sufficiently large even integer $h$, the weight $S$ of objects avoiding all bad conditions satisfies $$\\label{eq:block-sieve}\n S=X\\prod_{p\\le z}(1-g(p))\\bigl(1+O(e^{-h})\\bigr)\n   +O\\!\\left(\\sum_{d\\le z^{4h+2}}|r(d)|\\right).$$ Only the indicated primes and their squarefree products are used. The implied constants depend on $\\eta_0,C$, uniformly also when $h$ grows. For the fixed even choice $h=2$, there is an upper bound by a constant times $X\\prod(1-g(p))$ plus the same remainder sum.*\n\n*More precisely, the proof supplies polynomials $U$ and $V$ in the indicators of the bad conditions such that $$V\\le\\mathbf 1_{\\text{no bad condition}}\\le U,\\qquad U\\ge0.$$ Both have coefficients of absolute value at most one, supported on squarefree $d\\le z^{4h+2}$. The nonnegative overcount $U-\\mathbf 1_{\\text{no bad condition}}$ is dominated by the pointwise nonnegative polynomial $U-V$, whose absolute coefficients are also at most one and which has the same level bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-011.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Consider a finite set of objects with nonnegative weights. For some primes $p\\le z$, let a bad condition be specified. For every sufficiently large even integer $h$, the weight $S$ of objects avoiding all bad conditions satisfies $$\\label{eq:block-sieve}\n S=X\\prod_{p\\le z}(1-g(p))\\bigl(1+O(e^{-h})\\bigr)\n   +O\\!\\left(\\sum_{d\\le z^{4h+2}}|r(d)|\\right).$$ Only the indicated primes and their squarefree products are used. The implied constants depend on $\\eta_0,C$, uniformly also when $h$ grows. For the fixed even choice $h=2$, there is an upper bound by a constant times $X\\prod(1-g(p))$ plus the same remainder sum.*\n\n*More precisely, the proof supplies polynomials $U$ and $V$ in the indicators of the bad conditions such that $$V\\le\\mathbf 1_{\\text{no bad condition}}\\le U,\\qquad U\\ge0.$$ Both have coefficients of absolute value at most one, supported on squarefree $d\\le z^{4h+2}$. The nonnegative overcount $U-\\mathbf 1_{\\text{no bad condition}}$ is dominated by the pointwise nonnegative polynomial $U-V$, whose absolute coefficients are also at most one and which has the same level bound.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rough-sieve-polynomial",
        "exact_quote": "For the ordinary bad conditions $p\\mid n$ at primes $p\\le z$, let $U(n)=\\sum_{d\\mid n}\\lambda_d$ be the upper polynomial in [eq:sieve-polynomials](#eq:sieve-polynomials), and put $D=z^{4h+2}$. Then $$U(n)\\ge\\mathbf 1_{P^{-}(n)>z}\\ge0,\\quad\n |\\lambda_d|\\le1,\\quad\n \\lambda_d=0\\quad\\text{unless $d\\le D$ is squarefree},$$ and $$\\label{eq:rough-sieve-overcount}\n \\sum_{n\\in I\\cap\\mathbb N}\n \\bigl(U(n)-\\mathbf 1_{P^{-}(n)>z}\\bigr)\n \\ll |I|e^{-h}+D$$ for every finite interval $I\\subset[1,\\infty)$ and all sufficiently large even $h$. The bound is uniform in $h,z,I$. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:rough-sieve-harmonic}\n \\sum_d\\frac{|\\lambda_d|}{d}\n \\le\\prod_{p\\le z}(1+p^{-1})\\ll\\log(2z).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-011.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-transference",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $E>0$ there is $E_{\\rm id}>0$, depending only on $E$ and the fixed data in [def:prime-groups](#def:prime-groups), with the following property. Assume $$\\label{eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis}\n \\sup_{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R}\\lVert \\mathcal T(\\Theta)\\rVert\\le L^{-E_{\\rm id}}.$$ Then, for every sufficiently large fixed $J$, where the lower bound on $J$ may also depend on $m,C_1$, put $$R=\\lceil L^{0.52}\\rceil,\\qquad N=2R.$$ For every fixed $\\gamma>0$ and $I=[X,2X)$ with $x^\\gamma\\le X\\le x^{1/\\gamma}$, one has $$\\label{eq:transference-moment}\n \\frac1{|I\\cap\\mathbb Z|}\\sum_{n\\in I\\cap\\mathbb Z}\n \\langle u_n,(\\mathcal A\\mathcal A^*)^Ru_n\\rangle\n \\le L^{-EN},$$ for all sufficiently large $x$. Here $u_n$ is one on every physical state at position $n$ and zero elsewhere. The threshold for $x$ may depend on all fixed parameters, including $J,C_2,\n\\gamma$, and the bound is uniform subject to these parameters and [eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis](#eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis). No bound on $\\zeta$ is needed apart from the assumed ideal estimate.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-transference",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $E>0$ there is $E_{\\rm id}>0$, depending only on $E$ and the fixed data in [def:prime-groups](#def:prime-groups), with the following property. Here $u_n$ is one on every physical state at position $n$ and zero elsewhere. The threshold for $x$ may depend on all fixed parameters, including $J,C_2,\n\\gamma$, and the bound is uniform subject to these parameters and [eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis](#eq:ideal-norm-hypothesis). No bound on $\\zeta$ is needed apart from the assumed ideal estimate.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-transference-pairing",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses and choices of [thm:transference](#thm:transference), let $f\\in\\mathcal H_{\\rm ph}$ be supported on positions $I=[X,2X)$ and independent of the chosen marks, with value $f_n$ at position $n$. Suppose $$\\sup_{n\\in I}|f_n|\\le\\exp(O(\\sqrt L)),\\qquad\n \\lVert f\\rVert,\\lVert g\\rVert\\le X^{1/2}L^{C_3}.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:transference-pairing}\n |\\langle f,\\mathcal A g\\rangle|\n       \\ll XL^{-E+2C_3}.$$ Only the first endpoint must be independent of its mark list.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-transference-pairing",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses and choices of [thm:transference](#thm:transference), let $f\\in\\mathcal H_{\\rm ph}$ be supported on positions $I=[X,2X)$ and independent of the chosen marks, with value $f_n$ at position $n$.           "source_id": "PM-011.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-ideal",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $E_{\\rm id}>0$ and a fixed frequency exponent $C_4\\geq0$. There are fixed integers $m,J_0$ and a fixed constant $C_1$, depending only on these parameters, $\\ell$, and the prime-group bounds, with the following property. For every fixed $J\\geq J_0$, there is a family consisting of the raw pattern $$C_g=\\varnothing\\quad\\hbox{for every }g,\\qquad K_0=1,$$ and signed comparison patterns satisfying $$C_g=\\varnothing\\ (g\\in\\mathcal S),\\qquad\n |C_g|\\leq m\\ (g\\in\\mathcal B),\\qquad\n \\sum_\\nu\\sup|K_\\nu|\\leq L^{C_1},$$ such that the symmetrized ideal operator of [sec:transference](#sec:transference) satisfies $$\\label{eq:ideal-small-norm}\n \\sup_{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R,\\ |\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}}\n \\lVert \\mathcal T(\\Theta)\\rVert_{2\\to2}\\leq L^{-E_{\\rm id}}$$ for sufficiently large $x$. The coefficients and the family are independent of $\\Theta$ and of the individual value of $\\zeta$. Their defining parameters are independent of $J$; $J$ only supplies the available shared slots. The threshold for $x$ may depend on $J$.*\n\n*More precisely, designate $m$ of the first $J$ slots in each big group as probes, and split the big groups into two blocks. Each comparison frees nonempty sets $A$ and $B$ of designated probes in the respective blocks, and its coefficient has the form $$\\label{eq:ideal-comparison-coefficient}\n -(-1)^{|A|+|B|}\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\n                         \\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B).$$ Here $D_A,D_B$ are the products of the free labels used to form $D$, $X_A$ is the product of the target labels in $A$, and $X_B$ the product of the input labels in $B$. These coefficients use no shared labels and none of the last $\\ell$ labels. Each kernel is a finite sum of products of matched log-cell indicators and pairs of Dirichlet characters of conductor bounded by a fixed power of $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bilinear",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U,V\\geq1$. Suppose $a_n,b_t$ are supported in integer intervals of lengths at most $C U,C V$, respectively, where $C$ is fixed. If $$\\alpha=u/r+\\beta,\\qquad (u,r)=1,\\quad r\\geq1,\n \\qquad |\\beta|\\leq r^{-2},$$ then $$\\label{eq:integer-bilinear}\n \\left|\\sum_{n,t}a_nb_te\\!\\left(\\alpha nt\\right)\\right|\n \\ll_C \\lVert a\\rVert_{\\ell^2}\\lVert b\\rVert_{\\ell^2}\n \\left[U+(1+V/r)\\{U+r\\log(2r)\\}\\right]^{1/2}.$$ The containing intervals need not begin at $1$, and the coefficients may vanish on arbitrary subsets of them.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-011.1"
        }

        "exact_quote": "Fix $m$, $A_0>0$, and $C_4\\geq0$. There are fixed powers $Q,h^{-1},\\xi^{-1}$ of $L$, independent of $J$, such that the kernels in [eq:ideal-cell-kernel](#eq:ideal-cell-kernel) have the following property. Let $A,B$ be nonempty sets of slots, using at most $m$ slots per big group. Take independent label tuples for $D_A,X_A,D_B,X_B$ with the prescribed slot laws. If $f$ and $g$ are arbitrary $L^2$ tests on the tuples making up $X_B$ and $X_A$, respectively, then for every $\\alpha\\in\\mathbb R$ and $|\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-comparison}\n \\begin{split}\n \\Big|\\mathbb E\\,\\overline{f(X_B)}g(X_A)\n  \\big[&(X_AX_B)^{i\\zeta}e\\!\\left(\\alpha X_AX_B\\right)\\\\\n      &-\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B)\n                (D_AD_B)^{i\\zeta}e\\!\\left(\\alpha D_AD_B\\right)\\big]\\Big|\n \\leq L^{-A_0}\\lVert f\\rVert_2\\lVert g\\rVert_2.\n \\end{split}$$ Here notation such as $f(X_B)$ denotes a tuple test, not an assumption of dependence only on the product.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-shift",
        "exact_quote": "Fix all the group and endpoint bounds above. Fix $D_0,C>0$ and a fixed compact interval $[c_\\Psi,C_\\Psi]\\subset(0,\\infty)$. Suppose $0<|k|\\leq L^C$ is integral, $xL^{-C}\\leq Z\\leq xL^C$, $|a_1|,|a_2|\\leq L^C$, and $\\Psi$ is supported in that interval with $\\|\\Psi^{(j)}\\|_\\infty\\ll_j L^{C(j+1)}$ for every $j\\geq0$. There exists a fixed $B$, depending only on these bounds and $D_0$, such that [eq:discrepancy](#eq:discrepancy) implies $$\\label{eq:shift-bound}\n \\sum_{\\substack{z>0\\\\z+k>0}}\n \\frac{\\Psi(z/Z)}z\\,z^{ia_1}(z+k)^{ia_2}\n       \\overline{F_0(z)}G_0(z+k)W_\\ell(z)W_\\ell(z+k)\n       \\ll L^{-D_0}.$$ The bound is uniform over the indicated sequences, intervals, characters, frequencies, and cutoffs. It also holds when the discrepancy-bearing endpoint or its coefficients have been conjugated.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\tau>0$, $\\eta<1/4$, $C>0$, and $A>0$. Let $x^\\tau/2\\leq P'\\leq x^\\eta$, let $J'\\subset[P',2P']$ be an interval, and let $\\chi$ have modulus at most $L^C$. Suppose that $\\lvert \\alpha_m\\rvert,\\lvert \\beta_n\\rvert\\leq L^C$, and that $\\alpha_m=0$ unless $P^{-}(m)>W$. There is a fixed exponent $B$, depending only on these data and the fixed group and band constants, such that the discrepancy hypothesis in [eq:discrepancy](#eq:discrepancy) on $\\alpha$ implies $$\\label{eq:typeii-bound}\n \\sum_{\\substack{m\\asymp U,\\ n\\asymp V,\\ u\\geq1\\\\mn=2u+1}}\n      A(u)\\Psi(u/x)\\alpha_m\\beta_n\n       \\ll xL^{-D_*}.$$ Here the sequence to which the discrepancy hypothesis is applied includes the actual restriction $m\\asymp U$ and any additional interval restriction on $m$. Explicitly, for that restricted sequence one requires $$\\left|\\sum_{m\\in I}\\frac{\\alpha_m\\chi(m)m^{it}}m\\right|\n       \\leq L^{-B}\n \\quad\\left(I\\subset[1,x^2],\\quad\n       \\operatorname{mod}(\\chi)\\leq L^B,\\quad |t|\\leq L^B\\right).$$ There is no discrepancy hypothesis on $\\beta$. The constants are uniform over the sequences, scales, and interval restrictions satisfying these conditions.",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Totient fiber g(n)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $$g(n)=\#\{m\ge1:\varphi(m)=n\}$$ for the size of its fiber at $n$.
- **Extreme prime factors and smoothness** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): We write $P^{+}(n)$ and $P^{-}(n)$ for the largest and least prime factors of $n>1$, with $P^{+}(1)=1$ and $P^{-}(1)=\infty$.
- **Smooth and rough integers** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): An integer is $y$-smooth if $P^{+}(n)\le y$, and is rough above $y$ if $P^{-}(n)>y$.
- **Prime groups** ([source](#stmt-def-prime-groups)): Fix constants $$0<a<b<c<d<0.47,\qquad c_0,C_0,v_-,v_+>0, \qquad 0<q<1,\qquad \ell\in\mathbb N.$$ For each sufficiently large $x$, let the finite, pairwise disjoint sets of primes $\mathcal P_g$ be indexed by the disjoint sets $\mathcal S$ and $\mathcal B$, where $$c_0T\le \|\mathcal S\|,\|\mathcal B\|\le C_0T.$$ The small and big groups, respectively, satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \exp(L^a)\le p\le\exp(L^b)&\quad(p\in\mathcal P_g, \ g\in\mathcal S),\notag\\ \exp(L^c)\le p\le\exp(L^d)&\quad(p\in\mathcal P_g, \ g\in\mathcal B),\label{eq:prime-group-ranges}\\ v_-\le V_g:=\sum_{p\in\mathcal P_g}\frac1p&\le v_+, \qquad \mu_g(p):=\frac1{V_gp}.\notag \end{aligned}$$ Write $s=\|\mathcal S\|+\|\mathcal B\|$ and $$\omega_g(n)=\sum_{p\in\mathcal P_g}\mathbf 1_{p\mid n}, \qquad \omega(n)=\sum_g\omega_g(n) \quad(n\in\mathbb Z).$$ Thus divisibility at $n=0$ has its usual meaning. We call primes in $\bigcup_g\mathcal P_g$ group primes.
- **Ideal operator** ([source](#stmt-def-ideal-operator)): On the space $$\mathcal H_{\rm id} =L^2\left(\prod_{g\in\mathcal B}\mathcal P_g^M, \ \bigotimes_{g\in\mathcal B}\mu_g^{\otimes M}\right),$$ repetitions of prime values are allowed. For a real $\zeta$, the unsymmetrized ideal row operation for $\nu$ retains shared labels, samples all unshared target labels independently with laws $\mu_g$, and samples the auxiliary labels of $D_{\mathcal B}$ independently with the same laws. Its multiplier is $$K_\nu D_{\mathcal B}^{i\zeta}e\!\left(\Theta D_{\mathcal B}\right).$$ The sum over $\nu$, composed on both sides with big-group symmetrization, is denoted $\mathcal T(\Theta)$.
- **Physical operator** ([source](#stmt-def-physical-operator)): A physical state $(n,\mathbf p)$ consists of $n\in\mathbb Z$ and an ordered list $\mathbf p=(p_{g,i})_{g,1\le i\le M}$ such that, within each group, the labels are distinct and divide $n$. Give every state at $n$ mass $\prod_gV_g^{-M}$ and use counting measure in $n$. This defines $\mathcal H_{\rm ph}$. Fix an integer $k$ with $0<\|k\|\le L^{C_2}$. For pattern $\nu$, sample the auxiliary labels forming $D$ as above and move from $n$ to $n'=n+kD$. Sum over physical target states at $n'$ with the prescribed shared labels, with coefficient $\prod_g V_g^{-t_g}$ for each target. Forbid every auxiliary free label of $D$ from both endpoint lists. Auxiliary free labels may equal one another. Multiply this row action by $$\label{eq:physical-multiplier} K_\nu D^{i\zeta} q^{(\omega(n)-Ms)/2}q^{(\omega(n')-Ms)/2}.$$ The sum over patterns, symmetrized in all groups on both sides, is $\mathcal A$.
- **Lifespan of a big prime** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Consequently each represented big prime has exactly one *lifespan*: an interval containing all its active visits, possibly containing none.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-totient-fiber-lower | g(n) for infinitely many n | > | $n^{1-\varepsilon}$ | Any fixed positive epsilon; infinitely many n. |
| q-smooth-prime-count | number of primes p in the window with smooth predecessor | ≥ | $x^{1-o(1)}$ | The o(1) term may depend on delta; x tends to infinity. |
| q-fiber-upper-bound | g(n) | ≤ | $n^{1+\eta}$ | Implied constant depends on eta. |
| q-ideal-operator-norm | norm of the symmetrized ideal operator | ≤ | $L^{-E_{\rm id}}$ | Sufficiently large x; threshold may depend on J. |
| q-transference-moment | averaged high moment of the physical graph | ≤ | $L^{-EN}$ | Sufficiently large x and J; uniform subject to the ideal norm estimate. |
| q-shift-correlation | lifted shifted correlation sum | ≤ | $L^{-D_0}$ | Fixed D_0; discrepancy exponent B chosen accordingly. |
| q-type-ii-sum | Type II bilinear sum | ≤ | $xL^{-D_*}$ | Uniform over sequences and scales in the stated ranges. |
| q-type-i-remainders | sum of absolute congruence remainders | ≤ | $xL^{-D}+X_AL^{-18}$ | Fixed theta below one half. |
| q-candidate-mass-lower | candidate mass X_A | ≥ | $xL^{-C_A}$ | Constants independent of the rough slot band. |
| q-roughness-cutoff | roughness cutoff W | = | $W=\exp(\sqrt L)$ | x tends to infinity. |
| q-memory-truncation | memory size truncation B | = | $B=\lceil L^2\rceil$ | Applied before and after every operation. |
| q-group-exponent-cap | largest group exponent d | < | $0.47$ | Fixed constants. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-siegel-walfisz** [claim](#stmt-thm-sw) / [proof](#stmt-thm-sw): Quoted classical theorem, passed from the von Mangoldt form by partial summation. -> Prime counts in progressions to logarithmic-power moduli.
- **s-large-sieve** [claim](#stmt-thm-large-sieve) / [proof](#stmt-thm-large-sieve): Quoted classical theorem. -> Mean-square bound over primitive characters.
- **s-harmonic-characters** [claim](#stmt-cor-harmonic-sw) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-harmonic-sw): Sum Siegel–Walfisz over classes, then partial summation. -> Log-power saving for twisted harmonic prime sums.
- **s-divisor-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-divisor-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-divisor-moments): Euler product bound with Mertens, then counting. -> Coefficient energy bounds for convolutions.
- **s-dirichlet-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-dirichlet-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dirichlet-mean): Diagonal and off-diagonal integration, then Sobolev inequality. -> Continuous and discrete mean squares.
- **s-derivative-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-derivative-tests) / [proof](#stmt-lem-derivative-tests): Quoted classical exponential-sum tests. -> Cancellation for monotone phases.
- **s-fourier-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-separation): Repeated integration by parts and Fourier inversion. -> Separated phases with polylogarithmic mass.
- **s-block-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-sieve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sieve): Block inclusion–exclusion polynomials with independent-expectation comparison. -> Upper and lower sieve polynomials with level bound.
- **s-rough-polynomial** [claim](#stmt-lem-rough-sieve-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rough-sieve-polynomial): Specialize the block sieve to ordinary divisibility conditions. -> Majorant polynomial for rough integers.
- **s-physical-schur** [claim](#stmt-lem-physical-schur) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-physical-schur): Count ordered unshared lists and bound by a supremum per group. -> Absolute row and column bounds.
- **s-residue-replacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-transference-crt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transference-crt): Chinese remainder averaging with Bonferroni truncation. -> Independent-residue model for the path factor.
- **s-memory-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-memory-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-memory-identity): Lifespan expansion matched to local operators on the memory space. -> Exact operator-product form of the path sum.
- **s-memory-absolute** [claim](#stmt-lem-memory-absolute) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-memory-absolute): Weighted Schur test on absolute kernels with factorial adjoint measures. -> Uniform weighted row and column bounds.
- **s-rare-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-rare-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rare-transfer): Unique factorization shows few omission choices allow a promotion. -> Small norm for dirty edge terms.
- **s-clean-norm** [claim](#stmt-lem-clean-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-clean-norm): Condition on shared small labels, Fourier transform residues, reduce to the ideal operator. -> Clean edge norm with a power saving.
- **s-transference** [claim](#stmt-thm-transference) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-clean-norm-2): Inclusion–exclusion over birth equalities, large-rank and small-rank estimates. -> Averaged high moment of the physical graph.
- **s-transference-pairing** [claim](#stmt-cor-transference-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-transference-pairing): Block decomposition, spectral Hölder, trace bound on the positive semidefinite matrix. -> Pairing cancellation against mark-independent vectors.
- **s-bilinear** [claim](#stmt-lem-bilinear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bilinear): Cauchy–Schwarz and separation of phases near a rational. -> Integer bilinear estimate.
- **s-comparison-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-kernel): Dirichlet approximation, minor arcs by bilinear bound, major arcs by character kernels. -> Product-to-independent comparison with arbitrary precision.
- **s-ideal-operator** [claim](#stmt-thm-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-kernel-2): Efron–Stein decomposition over probes with alternating-sign cancellation. -> Small ideal operator norm at every frequency.
- **s-exceptional-times** [claim](#stmt-lem-exceptional-times) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exceptional-times): High moment of the small-prime polynomial with Sobolev inequality. -> Subpower count of exceptional unit intervals.
- **s-sparse-residual** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparse-residual): Gram matrix row sums using derivative tests, then Schur bound. -> Sparse mean square for the residual polynomial.
- **s-long-rough** [claim](#stmt-lem-long-rough-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-long-rough-polynomial): Sieve majorant plus iterated van der Corput differencing. -> Cancellation of the long rough factor at large frequencies.
- **s-shift-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-thm-shift) / [proof](#pm-h-38): Lift to the physical graph, remove shared labels, major/minor arcs, Mellin conversion, three time regimes. -> Shifted correlation cancellation.
- **s-type-ii** [claim](#stmt-thm-typeii) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-typeii): Smooth split of u, Cauchy–Schwarz, determinant parametrization into shifted endpoints. -> Type II estimate.
- **s-candidate-mass** [claim](#stmt-prop-candidate-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-candidate-mass): Factor harmonic masses and localize the product by one free prime. -> Candidate mass and pointwise bounds.
- **s-type-i** [claim](#stmt-prop-typei) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-typei): Character orthogonality with large sieve for large conductors and Siegel–Walfisz for small ones. -> Congruence distribution up to a level under one half.
- **s-proxy-discrepancy** [claim](#stmt-lem-proxy-discrepancy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-proxy-discrepancy): Block sieve on cells for rough counts; Siegel–Walfisz for nonprincipal characters. -> Discrepancy for test minus proxy.
- **s-proxy-densities** [claim](#stmt-lem-proxy-densities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-proxy-densities): Prime number theorem on cells, minimum-coordinate decomposition of the simplex, sieve upper bound. -> Density bounds and integral identity.
- **s-smooth-primes** [claim](#stmt-thm-smooth) / [proof](#pm-h-55): Pre-sieve, subtract unbalanced composites with Type II, bound balanced composites by an upper-bound sieve, then count primes. -> Many primes with smooth predecessors.
- **s-finite-fibers** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-totient-fibers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-totient-fibers): Divisibility restricts prime factors and exponents; lower bound for the totient gives the power bound. -> Finiteness and upper bound of fibers.
- **s-totient-fibers** [claim](#stmt-thm-totient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-totient-fibers-2): Products of subsets of smooth shifted primes and pigeonhole over few totient values. -> Infinitely many integers with large fibers.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-large-totient-fibers**: For every positive exponent deficit, the totient fiber size exceeds the corresponding power of n at infinitely many integers n. This is an infinitely-often statement about exceptional integers, not a statement about typical n.
- **inv-smooth-shifted-primes**: For each fixed smoothness exponent in the stated range, the number of primes in the dyadic-type window whose predecessor is smooth is at least a power of x with exponent one minus a vanishing term, where the vanishing term may depend on the exponent.
- **inv-parameter-order**: All constants are fixed before x tends to infinity, and auxiliary parameters are chosen in a specified order, so that no later choice feeds back into an earlier one.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-typical-integer-fibers**: Use the large-fiber theorem to size the preimage set of an arbitrary or random integer. -> The theorem only produces exceptional integers infinitely often; for general n the fiber can be far smaller, and only the elementary upper bound holds for all n.
- **anti-uniform-smoothness-sweep**: Let the smoothness exponent shrink with x and apply the prime count as if its error term were uniform. -> The vanishing term in the exponent depends on the fixed exponent, so the count is not controlled when the exponent varies with x.
- **anti-positive-proportion**: Cite the prime count as proof that a positive proportion of primes have smooth predecessors. -> A lower bound with exponent one minus a vanishing term does not give a positive limiting proportion.
- **anti-constructive-sampling**: Treat the argument as an efficient procedure for producing integers with huge totient fibers or smooth shifted primes. -> The fiber bound comes from a counting (pigeonhole) argument over products of primes, which asserts existence of some n without exhibiting it.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are asymptotic existence and counting statements (infinitely many n, at least x^{1-o(1)} primes) proved by analytic operator transference and a pigeonhole count over products of primes; the source states no finite procedure with explicit inputs and outputs, and its implied constants are not effective, so no runnable reference implementation or exact oracle can certify them.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results are asymptotic existence and distribution statements with constants fixed before the limit. They are not a library routine, so there is no function contract to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The probabilistic objects are proof devices (independent residues, marked prime lists, Haar measure). They are not a simulation model with a runnable step rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper supplies no executable procedure from which a tool could be built. The block sieve is stated as an inequality with an unspecified implied constant, not as a program. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The totient step is an existence argument by counting products of smooth shifted primes and pigeonholing over possible values. It selects no explicit integer by a finite procedure, so no complexity can be stated. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The memory space of pending prime particles is a Hilbert-space device inside a proof. It has no insertion, lookup or update operations with costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No computation, dataflow or arithmetic kernel appears. The bounds are analytic and only meaningful as the scale tends to infinity. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The count of primes with smooth predecessors is only a power-exponent lower bound with an o(1) that depends on the smoothness exponent. It gives no estimate of any concrete key-generation or factoring-attack cost. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no learning problem, data, loss or generalisation claim. Its high-moment operator estimates concern prime-labelled dilation graphs only. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or search is optimised. The only extremal statement is the exponent of the largest totient fibers, which is an existence result. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no nodes, messages or failure models. Parallel structure appears only as independent random labels inside operator-norm estimates. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home of the paper is analytic number theory, and it yields no engineering artefact outside the listed domains. Existence of large totient fibers is a mathematical conclusion only. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-infinitely-often-existence** (nonconstructive): The large totient fiber result is an infinitely-often existence statement obtained by counting products of smooth shifted primes and pigeonholing over possible totient values. It does not exhibit the integers, and it says nothing about typical integers. Blocked use: ['Sizing the preimage set of an arbitrary or random integer from the lower bound', 'Using the argument as a procedure that outputs an integer with a large fiber']
- **lim-delta-nonuniform** (hypothesis): The smooth shifted prime count holds for each fixed smoothness exponent in the stated range, and the vanishing term in the exponent may depend on that exponent. No uniformity as the exponent shrinks is asserted. Blocked use: ['Letting the smoothness exponent tend to zero with x while treating the error term as uniform', 'Using one threshold in x for all smoothness exponents']
- **lim-power-exponent-only** (formal_scope): The unconditional prime count gives only the full power exponent of the count. A positive limiting proportion is a stronger conclusion that is not proved. Blocked use: ['Citing the count as a positive density of primes with smooth predecessors', 'Deriving a proportion-based vulnerability estimate for key generation']
- **lim-ineffective-constants** (approximation): The Siegel–Walfisz input has constants that need not be effective, and these feed the later estimates, so the asymptotic bounds carry no explicit numerical thresholds. Blocked use: ['Extracting an explicit value of x beyond which the prime count bound holds', 'Using the bounds as runtime checks on prime counts']
- **lim-parameter-order** (hypothesis): All constants are fixed before x tends to infinity, and the auxiliary parameters are chosen in a stated order. Thresholds in x depend on all earlier fixed parameters. Blocked use: ['Reversing the order of parameter choices, for example choosing the mark length before the comparison kernels', 'Letting fixed constants grow with x']
- **lim-ideal-norm-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The transference bound for the physical graph is conditional on a small norm of the ideal operator at every additive frequency, and it requires a sufficiently large fixed slot count J. The moment bound is stated for sufficiently large x. Blocked use: ['Applying the moment bound without verifying the ideal operator norm hypothesis', 'Applying it with a small slot count']
- **lim-first-endpoint-independence** (edge_case): The pairing cancellation needs only the first endpoint to be independent of its mark list; the second endpoint needs only a norm bound. Blocked use: ['Applying the pairing estimate when the first endpoint depends on the chosen marks']
- **lim-discrepancy-first-endpoint** (hypothesis): The shifted correlation and Type II estimates require the discrepancy condition on the coefficient sequence of the first endpoint with a sufficiently large fixed exponent. No discrepancy hypothesis is imposed on the other sequence. Blocked use: ['Applying the Type II bound to a sequence that lacks the discrepancy condition', 'Assuming the discrepancy is transferred to a new coefficient sequence']
- **lim-group-ranges** (formal_scope): The prime group exponents are fixed constants below a stated cap, and each big group is the set of primes in an interval within the big range. Blocked use: ['Using groups with exponents beyond the cap', 'Using big groups that are not intervals of primes']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Erdős's conjecture on the largest fibers of Euler's totient function: for every <i>ε</i> &gt; 0, infinitely many positive integers <i>n</i> have more than $`n^{1-\varepsilon }`$ preimages. We also show that, for every fixed <i>δ</i> &gt; 0, there are at least $`x^{1-o(1)}`$ primes <i>p</i> in $`2x\lt p\le5x`$ whose predecessors have no prime factor exceeding <i>x</i><sup><i>δ</i></sup>.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Euler’s totient function can take the same value at many different integers. Write $$g(n)=\#\{m\ge1:\varphi(m)=n\}$$ for the size of its fiber at $n$. We prove the following result.

<a id="stmt-thm-totient"></a>
<div id="thm:totient" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every real $\varepsilon>0$, there are infinitely many positive integers $n$ such that $$g(n)>n^{1-\varepsilon}.$$*

</div>

resolves positively Erdős’s conjecture on the largest fibers of Euler’s totient function. Pomerance (Pomerance 1980, 84) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Pomerance1980](#ref-Pomerance1980)] formulates the conjecture as $C=1$, where $C$ is the supremum of the exponents $c$ for which $g(n)>n^c$ infinitely often, and attributes it to Erdős (Erdős 1956) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Erdos1956](#ref-Erdos1956)]. An elementary upper bound is $g(n)\ll_\eta n^{1+\eta}$ for every $\eta>0$; we recall it in [sec:totients](#sec:totients). Thus the power exponent of $n$ in the theorem is optimal.

The arithmetic input is a result about the prime factors of the predecessor of a prime. For $m>1$, let $P^+(m)$ be its largest prime factor. Erdős’s work on totient multiplicities and smooth shifted primes dates to (Erdős 1935, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Erdos1935](#ref-Erdos1935)]. The underlying construction takes products of many primes whose predecessors have few available prime factors: many distinct products then have the same totient. The expectation that $p-1$ can have all its prime factors smaller than every fixed power of $p$ was recorded in (Erdős 1956) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Erdos1956](#ref-Erdos1956)]; see also (Lichtman 2022, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Lichtman2022](#ref-Lichtman2022)]. We prove a quantitative form.

<a id="stmt-thm-smooth"></a>
<div id="thm:smooth" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *For every fixed $0<\delta<1/4$, as $x\to\infty$, <a id="eq:main-smooth"></a>$$\label{eq:main-smooth}
 \#\{p:\ p\text{ prime},\ 2x<p\le5x,
                   \ P^+(p-1)\le x^\delta\}
 \ge x^{1-o(1)}.$$ The quantity $o(1)$ may depend on $\delta$.*

</div>

In particular, for every $\varepsilon>0$ there are infinitely many primes $p$ with $P^+(p-1)\le p^\varepsilon$. This resolves the smooth-predecessor conjecture positively as well. The count in [eq:main-smooth](#eq:main-smooth) also holds for every fixed $\delta>0$, by using a smaller positive exponent when necessary. No uniformity as $\delta\to0$ is asserted in [thm:smooth](#thm:smooth) or needed for [thm:totient](#thm:totient).

There is a substantial literature on fixed smoothness exponents. Baker and Harman (Baker and Harman 1998, sec. 1, Theorem 1 and Corollary 1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BakerHarman1998](#ref-BakerHarman1998)] obtained exponent $0.2961$ for shifted primes, with the corresponding totient multiplicity exponent $0.7039$. This improved Friedlander’s threshold $1/(2\sqrt e)+\varepsilon$; their introduction also describes preceding work of Pomerance, Balog, and Fouvry–Grupp. Lichtman (Lichtman 2022, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Lichtman2022](#ref-Lichtman2022)] proved a lower bound $x/(\log x)^C$ for $x<p\le2x$ and $P^+(p-1)\le x^\beta$ whenever $$\beta>\frac{15}{32\sqrt e}=0.284311\ldots,$$ and obtained multiplicity exponent $0.7156$. These results concern fixed positive exponents. The expected Dickman law predicts a positive proportion of primes for every such exponent; see Granville (Granville 2008, sec. 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Granville2008](#ref-Granville2008)]. Bharadwaj and Rodgers (Bharadwaj and Rodgers 2026, Theorem 7, Proposition 2, and Conjecture 5) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BharadwajRodgers2026](#ref-BharadwajRodgers2026)] prove the full Poisson–Dirichlet law for sequences satisfying their regularity and congruence-uniformity conditions together with level-one distribution. For shifted primes this gives the prediction under the Elliott–Halberstam conjecture; their Proposition 2 establishes distribution level one half and the other conditions unconditionally. Our unconditional bound $x^{1-o(1)}$ gives the full power exponent of the count for every fixed positive smoothness exponent. A positive limiting proportion is a stronger conclusion. The final passage from plentiful smooth shifted primes to large totient fibers belongs to the method of Erdős and Pomerance; Pomerance states the relevant transfer explicitly in (Pomerance 1980, Theorem B) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Pomerance1980](#ref-Pomerance1980)]. We give the needed product-and-pigeonhole proof in full.

Smooth predecessors also enter the construction of Carmichael numbers by Alford, Granville and Pomerance (Alford, Granville, and Pomerance 1994) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[AlfordGranvillePomerance1994](#ref-AlfordGranvillePomerance1994)], where their supply is combined with a separate distribution theorem for primes in arithmetic progressions. This is one reason that estimates for shifted smoothness have applications beyond the factorization problem itself. The contribution here includes the general graph and kernel theorems that produce the arithmetic lower bound, as well as the optimal exponent for totient fibers.

These graph and ideal-kernel estimates also serve as inputs to the companion paper (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[PrimePredecessors](#ref-PrimePredecessors)], where a separate determinant-graph estimate and prime-extraction argument yield the full Poisson–Dirichlet law for prime predecessors.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The analytic mechanism

The main obstacle is to detect primality in a sequence whose predecessors have a strongly constrained factorization. Congruence counts provide an initial sieve, and a further bilinear cancellation estimate permits the composite contribution to be removed. This division of work is familiar from prime-detecting sieves; compare Friedlander and Iwaniec (Friedlander and Iwaniec 1998, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[FriedlanderIwaniec1998](#ref-FriedlanderIwaniec1998)]. Our coefficient and range hypotheses are stated and verified below for the particular weight used here.

We count primes of the form $2u+1$, where $u$ has a prescribed factorization over many logarithmic scales. Some factors lie in two separated ranges of subpower-sized primes, divided into small and big groups. Their weights mark distinct prime divisors. The functions attached to an integer depend only on the part of $u$ outside these groups; the marked weights supply the divisibility labels for the dilation graph. Other factors occupy a geometric sequence of size bands, which permits a divisor of $u$ to be chosen close to a specified size. One band contains a long factor ranging over rough integers, meaning integers with no prime factor below a prescribed cutoff.

The main new analytic ingredient is a transference theorem for weighted dilation graphs. A physical state consists of an integer and ordered lists of distinct group-prime divisors. An edge has shift $kD$ for an integer $k\ne0$, where $D$ is a product of primes shared by its endpoint lists, together with independent free prime factors. The corresponding ideal operator acts on the big-group lists: its labels are independent, with probabilities proportional to $1/p$, and repetitions are allowed. The list length is sufficiently large but fixed as $x$ tends to infinity. A small ideal operator norm yields an averaged high moment of the physical graph, and hence cancellation in pairings whose first endpoint is independent of its mark list.

The transference proof averages the integer root by the Chinese remainder theorem. Repeated divisibility queries then pay a reciprocal prime factor only once; a memory retains their congruences between active uses. Averaging the omitted small marks makes transfers between memory and the active lists rare. A rank expansion restores distinct prime births while preserving cancellation on most edges: many independent birth equalities supply reciprocal-prime savings, whereas a small equality rank affects only a small fraction of the edges. The theorem applies to arbitrary signed kernels satisfying its explicit complexity and norm hypotheses.

Related divisibility-graph methods have a substantial history. Matomäki, Radziwiłł and Tao (Matomäki, Radziwiłł, and Tao 2016, Theorem 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MatomakiRadziwillTao2016](#ref-MatomakiRadziwillTao2016)] studied connectivity of a graph with prime-divisibility edges. Tao’s entropy-decrement argument (Tao 2016) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Tao2016](#ref-Tao2016)] replaces such divisibility conditions by their mean at a suitable scale. Helfgott and Radziwiłł (Helfgott and Radziwiłł 2021, sec. 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[HelfgottRadziwill2021](#ref-HelfgottRadziwill2021)] study centered adjacency operators through signed closed-walk moments, with repeated primes creating dependent congruences. Pilatte (Pilatte 2026, Theorem 2.4 and Section 5) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Pilatte2026](#ref-Pilatte2026)] develops this approach for shifts that are products of primes. These works explain the roles of centering, long moments, and repeated-label bookkeeping. Our graph also carries ordered active lists, and its comparison with an independent-label operator requires the explicit lifespan and factorial-memory construction proved in [sec:transference](#sec:transference).

The independent-label norm is made small by a comparison construction. Logarithmic localization and character kernels allow shared prime products to be replaced by independent products. The big groups are split into two blocks, with a bounded number of selected coordinates in each group. After averaging the list coordinates unused by the multiplier, a coordinate decomposition isolates components that have mean zero in all selected coordinates of one block. Permutation symmetry makes these components small, and signed comparison terms cancel the other components up to a controlled error. For each prescribed fixed accuracy, the construction gives kernels uniform in the additive frequency, with complexity fixed before the length of the marked lists is chosen.

Applying transference to the multiplicatively invariant endpoints reduces a fixed shifted correlation to averages over larger shifts. Products of free primes control the minor arcs. On the major arcs, a marked small prime and the compulsory long rough-integer factor control the local Fourier energy. A low-complexity character and Mellin discrepancy condition supplies the remaining cancellation. The argument permits arbitrary bounded residual coefficients.

These shifted correlations yield a Type II estimate for $mn=2u+1$. A probabilistic split of the geometric prime bands chooses a divisor $e$ of $u$ close to the scale of $m$. After Cauchy–Schwarz, off-diagonal factorizations become shifted endpoints by an exact determinant identity. The Type II estimate then permits primality and roughness tests on cofactors to be replaced by elementary rough proxies. Congruence estimates and a weighted sieve remove the composite values of $2u+1$; a separate upper bound treats the small balanced range.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Organization and dependencies

fixes the conventions and proves the elementary sieve used later. The central transference estimate is proved in [sec:transference](#sec:transference), and the independent-label kernels are constructed in [sec:ideal](#sec:ideal). Together they lead to the shifted-correlation theorem in [sec:shifts](#sec:shifts). The Type II reduction occupies [sec:typeii](#sec:typeii). The prime extraction and all its parameter choices are completed in [sec:primes](#sec:primes). [sec:totients](#sec:totients) finishes the proof of [thm:totient](#thm:totient). The main implications are summarized in [fig:proof-dependencies](#fig:proof-dependencies).

The sieve geometry is fixed first, the required analytic accuracies next, and the discrepancy precision of the cofactor proxies last. The relevant theorems state this choice order, and the prime extraction verifies it. In particular, the needed discrepancy is established independently of the estimate that uses it.

<figcaption>Main result dependencies. The auxiliary branch includes candidate mass, Type I distribution, proxy discrepancy, the elementary sieve, and the separate balanced-range bound. Arrows record the implications used in the proof; the order of choosing constants is described in the text.</figcaption>
</figure>
